Compose-vs-chart env asymmetry measured: no finding.

* **AU, 2026-09-13 — every vault key path in a log line renders through ONE shared redactor.** The worker's `AuditingProvider` logged `secret.resolve path="oauth/gmail/<user_id>/<EMAIL>/access_token"` at INFO; MCP-988's `redact_oauth_path_for_log` was `pub(crate)` in `talos-oauth`, never swept. **43** tracing statements on main with `key_path` / `vault_path` / bare-`path` fields, 9 via the helper, 34 raw. ONE home `talos_workflow_job_protocol::redact_vault_path_for_log` (beside `vault_path_permitted`) hashes the FOURTH segment of any `oauth/…` path with ≥4 segments whatever the leaf (the old helper matched only five-part `access_token` paths) — so the same credential's `refresh_token` twin and talos-google-calendar's four-segment prefix were invisible to it; `redact_oauth_provider_key_for_log` is the bare-field form. **Check 91** gates the field names (43 → 0). **Stated limit**: the 8-line window vouches for a redactor named, not applied — the behavioural guard is the capture test in `talos-secrets`. Worker field is `key_path` (was `path`). Folded: `📩 Received WASM log from NATS topic` is DEBUG. **Not caught, stated**: PII under a field named neither path nor key (Gmail `account`; 1 real of 15 `email` hits, fixed by hand).

* **AV, 2026-09-13 — `TalosAuditChainUnverifiable` split on the code's own partition.** Measured over 7 days: 3.7 h firing, five increments, every one `empty_chain`, every one a single job whose worker died mid-flight, ZERO deployment-wide reasons. `TalosAuditChainUnverifiable` now selects only the `ChainVerifyErrorKind::aborts_sweep` set (`access_denied` / `no_such_bucket` / `no_credentials`; unchanged threshold, `for: 0m`, warning); `TalosAuditChainJobsUnverifiable` is the complement as a RATIO over `talos_audit_chain_jobs_swept_total{outcome}` (pre-seeded over `JobChainOutcome::ALL`) — `> 25 %`, floor of five in 2 h, `for: 5m`. Selectors pinned to the enum at compile time (`alert_selectors_match_the_aborts_sweep_partition`); the seed list pinned equal to `JobChainOutcome::ALL`. Mutation that SURVIVED, then closed: `> 0.25 → > 0` passed every quiet case until a 6-of-110 case existed. Stated, not fixed: the WORM anchor is written at job end, so every worker restart with a job in flight mints one `empty_chain`; reporting it per job is correct.

* **AW, 2026-09-13 — the `AUDIT_LEDGER` JetStream stream is bounded to 30 days, in place.** Redis: 178 keys, all with TTL, 3.8 MB — no finding. JetStream: `StreamConfig { name, subjects, ..Default::default() }` (`Limits`, no `max_age`/`max_msgs`/`max_bytes`) held 58 978 messages / 31 MB since 2026-07-08, all acked, ~0.5 MB/day. ONE `audit_ledger_stream_config` with `talos_audit_ledger::AUDIT_LEDGER_STREAM_MAX_AGE` (30 days); `ensure_bounded_stream` applies it IN PLACE (`get_or_create_stream` never updates); retention POLICY cannot change on an existing stream (`Limits` ↔ `WorkQueue` refused), so the bound is an AGE. **Cost, stated**: `max_age` expires unacked messages too — a subscriber down >30 days loses what aged out; `TalosAuditChainJobsUnverifiable` (AV) makes it loud; gauge `talos_audit_ledger_consumer_pending` shows filling. A byte cap with `discard = Old` was REJECTED (drops the OLDEST unshipped first, silently). Dropping the backlog sample is INVISIBLE to check 58 (`.set()` in a helper — its stated wrapper limit). **Not alerted**: no baseline yet.

* **AX, 2026-09-13 — MCP agent-token auth refusals are counted and logged.** `mcp_auth_middleware`'s six refusal sites had no log and no counter (invisible below 60 req/min/IP). Every exit is a `McpAuthRefusal` variant (`RateLimited` / `MissingToken` / `UnknownToken` / `InvalidToken` / `UnscopedAgent` / `Error`); `authenticate_mcp_request` decides, `report_mcp_auth_refusal` counts on `talos_mcp_auth_total{outcome}` (seven values, `McpAuthOutcome::ALL`, pre-seeded) and logs — unknown/invalid token WARN under `talos_audit` as `mcp_auth_refused`, missing token DEBUG; the limiter joins `talos_rate_limit_hits_total{type="mcp_auth"}`. **Decisions**: the CALLER still gets one 401 for the three token outcomes (a reason split is a token-existence oracle); a REVOKED token is `unknown_token` by construction — deliberately no `revoked` value; `invalid_token` (lookup hash and bcrypt hash disagree — a corrupted row, never a guess) kept distinct; `error` IS a verdict, unlike `ApiKeyValidation`. **No alert, deliberately** — no baseline yet. **Stated limits**: log lines untested; `error` driven only by the unit test. `docs/security/operational-runbook.md` §3.4 names the series.

* **AY, 2026-09-14 — the engine's OAuth repair re-dispatched one `job_id` at attempt 0, minting false tamper verdicts.** The one-shot OAuth repair (#664) re-dispatched a CLONE of the `DispatchJob` with the same `job_id` and `dispatch_attempt` 0, so the worker opened a second chain in the first dispatch's partition; `talos_oauth_reactive_refresh_total{outcome="repaired"}` moved twice since 2026-08-29 — every repair ever recorded produced a false tamper verdict. Now `DispatchJob.dispatch_attempt_base` (default 0, first send byte-identical on the wire) is the first send's attempt; the NATS dispatcher counts both re-sign sites up from it; the repair starts at `DispatchJob::redispatch_attempt_base()` = `base + max_retries + 1` — ONE home. Deploy: no new wire field, any rolling order is safe; a pre-AY controller keeps producing the false verdict. Not changed, stated: the two historical prefixes keep failing if re-swept (forward-only); the alert text is unchanged — a `DuplicateSequence` inside one partition IS substitution evidence. No lint: population one re-dispatch site. Recon: the 30-day `AUDIT_LEDGER` stream and `module_execution_logs` outlive container restarts.

* **AZ, 2026-09-14 — an unchanged ML example re-append no longer rewrites rows or touches `ml_datasets.updated_at`, so hourly re-distills stop minting `ml_model_versions`.** Measured: ~30 versions/day, no DELETE anywhere; 129 of `ops-severity`'s 162 evaluations in 7 days identical once `unmet` order was normalised (inbox-classifier 2 of 62); the 1 043 kNN versions store NO artifact; 5 of 174 LR artifacts are byte duplicates. `ml_examples.content_fingerprint` (migration `20260914120000`, nullable, no backfill) = `content_identity::row_content_fingerprint`, HMAC under the ML content purpose key over domain label + dataset id + text; `DO UPDATE` fires only when `EXAMPLE_UPSERT_CHANGES` holds (ONE constant); the touch only on insert/change/eviction (`enforce_growth_cap` returns the evicted count); dedupe skipped when nothing was written; `evaluate_policy` iterates classes SORTED. **Security decisions**: DATASET-SCOPED fingerprint, not `content_key` (cross-tenant identical text unlinkable); a key-resolution failure is a `None` fingerprint → every row writes, never a skipped update. Embedding VALUES not compared (re-embedding measured bit-identical). **Stated**: `insert_prepared` / MCP `ml_append_examples` `stored` count rows INSERTED or CHANGED; the distill log carries `submitted`. Seams: existing rows rewrite ONCE; a KEK rotation is the same bounded seam. **Not changed**: the 1 232 existing versions (retention is a separate decision); the embedding backfill / grandfather writers. **Measured SURVIVOR** M11 (dedupe on every append): performance only; the live guard is the dedupe CTE's `pg_stat_statements` call count.

* **BA, 2026-09-14 — the Vault KEK token is renewed; production REFUSES an `Expiring` one.** No `renew-self` call existed (measured: a 45 s periodic token expired to a 403), so an installer `KEK_PROVIDER=vault` cluster lost its KEK path on day 32. Latent (dev runs `KEK_PROVIDER=env`). `TokenLifetime`: `NonExpiring` / `Periodic` / `RenewableBounded` / `Expiring` (`ttl`/`renewable` REQUIRED). Operator decision 2026-09-14: production refuses `Expiring`, deliberately no escape hatch (the token is read once — a rotated `VAULT_TOKEN_FILE` is never seen); `RenewableBounded` → boot ERROR. `run_token_renewal` renews at boot, then at a third of remaining TTL, ≤ hourly. `talos_vault_token_renewals_total{outcome=renewed|capped|failed}` LOOP-seeded; `talos_vault_token_ttl_seconds{lifetime}` unseeded. `BackgroundTask::VaultTokenRenewal`: new `Declined(NotNeeded)`; non-renewable → `LoopEnded` (a FINDING). `TalosVaultTokenRenewalFailing` (critical, `for: 15m`); `TalosVaultTokenCapped` (warning). `talos-secrets-manager/tests/vault_token_renewal_live.rs` is `ci-ungated`. Not changed: the init Job's placeholder-only patch; AppRole re-login for bounded tokens. No lint (population one).

* **BB, 2026-09-14 — `tool_search`'s `TOOL_GROUPS` and 19 prose sites named tools that do not exist.** 10 of 58 `TOOL_GROUPS` entries unadvertised (`pause_webhook`, `resume_webhook`, `test_webhook`, `rollback_version`, `compare_versions`, `remove_node`, `create_sandbox`, `compile_sandbox`, `compile_and_add_module`, `update_workflow`), `get_execution_status` twice; 19 prose sites (`infer_workflow_input_schema`, `get_approval_queue`, `analyze_failure`, `get_execution_delta` ×2, `run_workflow_hygiene`, `update_workflow` ×2, `reinstall_module_from_catalog` ×2, `set_secret` ×7, `rotate_secret` ×2). **General prose detector built, MEASURED, REJECTED**: ~10 % precision (21 hits / 2 real over 3 819 schema strings; 18 / ~7 workspace-wide), misses `infer_workflow_input_schema`. `TOOL_GROUPS` is pinned EXHAUSTIVELY instead. M11 (lexer continuation-stripping branch) first SURVIVED; the branch was DELETED rather than tested. **Measured and not changed**: an undefined-Makefile-variable detector is 100 % precise but population two — no check number; `fix_type: "rotate_secret"` is a category label. No per-route request series exists — recorded, not built. `--count` stays 91.

* **BC, 2026-09-14 — LaunchAgent PATHs are DERIVED from where the installing shell resolves each tool.** `scripts/drills/schedule.sh` and `scripts/offhost-backup/schedule.sh` hardcoded `PATH=/usr/local/bin:/opt/homebrew/bin:…`; rustup's default is `~/.cargo/bin`, so both were broken from day one while `make drill-schedule-status` said `✓ scheduled`. ONE home `scripts/lib/launchd-path.sh`: `launchd_path_for TOOL…` (`type -P`, then the base list) REFUSES the install naming an unresolved tool; schedulers declare `SCHEDULED_TOOLS` (drill `cargo docker`, off-host `cargo aws`), gain `render`; `status` reads the INSTALLED plist's PATH via `plutil` and names the missing tool. **Decisions**: DERIVED, never widened by adding `~/.cargo/bin` to the constant; no wholesale shell-PATH copy; refusal at install, not a WARN. `scripts/tests/launchd-path-test.sh` SKIPS LOUDLY on Linux for the `plutil` half. **Stated limits**: proves a tool RESOLVES, not a complete environment — no off-host/age-passphrase vars propagate, so a scheduled `--source b2` drill cannot run (recorded, not fixed); shims needing shell env (`RUSTUP_HOME`) out of range; nothing re-derives after install.

* **BD, 2026-09-14 — `execution_cost_rollup` joins the tier-four reaper on its own 90-day clock.** Measured: 59 023 rows / 24 MB, ~9 000 rows a week, swept by nothing. Now reaped by `reap_execution_side_tables` (5 000-row `SKIP LOCKED` batches, 6-hourly, `SideTableReap::execution_cost_rollup`) at `EXECUTION_COST_ROLLUP_RETENTION_DAYS` = 90, not the 60-day execution lifetime, because `get_workflow_performance_report` accepts `days` up to 90; `node_fuel_history`'s clamp moved 365 → 90. Decisions: a CONSTANT, deliberately not a knob; no `recorded_at` index (batched delete ~13 ms over 59 k rows). Stated: the demoted `last_child_activity_at` proxy now reads `null` past 90 days; its removal is scheduled once `ledger_since` predates the 30-day window (on or after 2026-10-06). Textual pin `cost_rollup_readers_never_ask_past_the_retention_window`; a new wider reader elsewhere is not caught. Measured SURVIVOR: the `truncated` flag ignoring the rollup's batch cap (needs > 100 000 qualifying rows; the log field is untested). Not changed: other readers' windows; `talos_cost_attribution::get_actor_cost_report` has no caller (recorded).

* **BE, 2026-09-14 — the demoted `last_child_activity_at` proxy (+ `DORMANT_CHILD_ACTIVITY_CAVEAT`) removed eight days early.** `ledger_since` is 2026-09-06 14:37; of five dormant workflows the two ledger-recorded children (6 and 18 runs) needed nothing from the proxy, the other three carried July timestamps outside the window, and the population it could still help (fuel activity between window start and `ledger_since`, no run) was ZERO. `attach_child_run_evidence` returned early on `None` and rendered nothing; it now takes `is_child` and renders `last_child_run_at` / `child_runs_since_ledger` / `ledger_since` as null plus `CHILD_LEDGER_NOT_READ_NOTE` for a CHILD whose ledger read failed, nothing for a non-child (`child_runs: None` means two things by row). The dormant query loses its correlated `MAX(recorded_at)` subselect. RFC 0012 carries a dated update; the `EXECUTION_COST_ROLLUP_RETENTION_DAYS` comment no longer describes the proxy.

* **BF, 2026-09-14 — the execution pause (`system_settings` row `execution_paused`) could never be set and was read by almost nothing; ONE home now, leaf crate `talos-execution-pause`.** Two byte-identical writers bound TEXT into `jsonb NOT NULL` (check 88's stated bind-TYPE limit, first live instance). Unread by the scheduler (1 982 of 2 951 runs/week, 67 %), the Gmail continuation (952, 32 %), the webhook router, `retry`, GraphQL `testWorkflow`: under 1 % of dispatch would have paused. Latent in use (no pause/resume call in 30 days). Home: three-valued read (`Running`/`Paused`/`Unreadable`; a non-boolean REFUSES), writer `to_jsonb($2::boolean)`, `#[must_use] gate_start(executor, path)`; both repository copies DELETED. **Defer, don't drop (operator decision 2026-09-14)**: `create_execution_under_concurrency_limit` reads the flag FIRST in-transaction → `ConcurrencyAdmission::ExecutionsPaused(reason)` / `BatchAdmission::paused`; the scheduler reads BEFORE the claim, claims nothing, does NOT spend the boot flag, re-arms via `rearm_schedule_deferred_by_pause` (`next_trigger_at = NOW()`, never re-enabling a disabled row); webhook → 503 + `Retry-After: 60`, dedup claim released, no DLQ; Gmail push gates BEFORE the always-200 cursor task, 503 for redelivery (a pause beyond subscription retention, 7 days default, loses what ages out — stated); `retry` gains the gate. `talos_execution_pause_refusals_total{path,reason}` (`PauseGatePath` 8 × `PauseRefusal` 2, pre-seeded). **No alert** — a refusal is the pause working. **Stated behaviour changes**: an unrecognised stored flag now refuses every start; `retry` refuses while paused; `OrchestrationError::ExecutionPaused` carries the reason. **Not gated here, stated**: GraphQL `testWorkflow`, `handoff`, approval/suspension continuation resumes, `test_subworkflow_contract`, GCal/GCP push. **Excluded by design**: crash-recovery resumes, sub-workflows of a running parent, chained workflows. **Unmeasured, stated**: removing the `trigger`/`replay` entry gates leaves tests green (row-creation backstop); `gate_start`'s counter covered only by the recorder unit test; call-site pins textual.

* **BG, 2026-09-14 — the execution pause gates its remaining seven start paths, defer-don't-drop.** **LATENT on this fleet**: `workflow_approval_gates` and `workflow_suspensions` 0 rows ever, handoff 0, module-bound pushes 0 in 7 days, 11 test executions. The check sits BEFORE the consuming (resolve/claim) statement: MCP `resolve_approval_gate` and the approve LINK only for an approval naming a continuation (`talos_continuation_trigger::resolution_starts_work`); MCP `resume_workflow_by_correlation_id` UNCONDITIONALLY before its claim (continuation-less resumes refused too); the CALLBACK after a peek (race admits, stated). Pushes share `talos_execution_pause::push_admission(executor, starts_work, path) -> PushAdmission { Admit, Defer(reason), DeferReadFailed }` (`#[must_use]`): Calendar (`gcal_webhook_starts_work`) and GCP (`gcp_push_starts_work`) gate BEFORE dedup/SETNX and spawned tasks. Entry gates: handoff, GraphQL `testWorkflow`, MCP `test_subworkflow_contract`. `PauseGatePath` is 13. (`graphql_test`, `handoff`, `continuation`, `gcal_push`, `gcp_push` added; all 26 pairs pre-seeded). Refusals say the record is still pending and the action can be repeated (`resolution_deferred_message`); the link and callback answer 503 + `Retry-After`. **Stated**: the Calendar and GCP handlers' own 503 is pinned, not driven. **Still not gated, by design**: crash-recovery resumes, sub-workflows of a running parent, chained workflows. **Unmeasured, stated**: handoff, GraphQL and contract gates are textual pins only.

* **BH, 2026-09-15 — an approval decision on `execution_approvals` is final.** `decide_execution_approval_scoped` (GraphQL `approveExecution`/`denyExecution`) lacked the `AND status = 'pending'` guard `update_execution_approval_decision` had (same-tenant, via `resumeWorkflow`). Population: 6 decided rows, 0 pending. **ONE home is the database**: migration `20260915100000` adds `trg_execution_approvals_decision_final` (`BEFORE UPDATE`), refusing any change to `status` / `decided_at` / `decided_by` / `reason` once `status <> 'pending'` (SQLSTATE 23514) for EVERY writer; DELETE unblocked. Deliberately NOT named `trg_%_immutable`. GraphQL returns `ApprovalDecisionWrite { Decided, AlreadyDecided { status }, NotFound }` (`#[must_use]`, `ApprovalDecision { Approved, Denied }`): the OWNER is told the decision is final, others still "not found". **Not changed, recorded**: the two-step decide-then-resume UI flow; the MCP/link writer. **SURVIVOR**: dropping `w.user_id = $2` from the already-decided read passed (the FORCE'd `workflows` RLS policy under `begin_user_scoped` hid the row); pinned by an unscoped-transaction test. **Unmeasured, stated**: resolver rendering (no GraphQL harness).

* **BI, 2026-09-15 — the disk preflight prints reclaimable figures beside its remedies; the `builder prune` flag has ONE home.** The "0 B from `--keep-storage 20GB`" explanation was measured and REFUTED twice: plain `docker builder prune -f` reclaimed 21.04 GB on the same cache, and `--keep-storage` is identical to `--reserved-space` with or without `-a`. The 0 B is UNEXPLAINED and stated as such. Changed: `docker image prune -f` printed and run by `make clean` FIRST; `reclaimable now:` figures behind a 3 s deadline (`TALOS_UP_DISK_INFO_DEADLINE_TENTHS`), omitted never invented, no extra call on the healthy path; `scripts/lib/docker-reclaim.sh::docker_prune_reserve_flag` (`--reserved-space` where `--help` lists it, else `--keep-storage`) used by the preflight (`-af … 20GB`) and `make clean` (`-af … 8gb`). Disclosed side effect: the plain prune removed the two cargo exec cache mounts (3.8 GB). `doctor.sh`, `QUICKSTART.md` and the CLAUDE.md recipe keep plain `docker builder prune -f`, which DOES reclaim — unchanged.

* **BJ, 2026-09-15 — a post-pause backlog is logged as a pause, not missed polls.** `observe_execution_pause` returns whether the PREVIOUS poll was deferred; `classify_backlog_report(phase, batch_len, previous_deferred)` yields `Startup` / `CatchupAfterPause` / `CatchupMissedPolls` / `None`; `log_backlog` emits `CatchupAfterPause` at INFO as `scheduler_pause_backlog`; missed polls stay WARN (`scheduler_catchup_backlog`). Deliberately unchanged: the `phase` label and the backlog permit. Stated limits: a host suspend during a pause reads as the pause lifting; the Gmail push deferral was not exercised; `admin_event_log` got no pause events because `pause_executions` refused a non-platform-admin caller.

* **BK, 2026-09-15 — check 88's PREPARE probe refuses a run that never reached the database.** Measured on main: wrong password, missing database, closed port, unresolvable host all exited 0 with output identical to a good run (psql's merged stderr defeated `returncode != 0 and not stdout`); CI unaffected. `read_probe_output(stdout, returncode, probe_names)` is the one classifier: a run counts only if psql exited 0 and echoed EVERY probe marker plus `@@@end`; else exit 2, `✗ the PREPARE probe could not run`. `scripts/test-integration.sh` runs a negative probe at a closed port requiring exit 2. **Stated limit**: the wrapper's exit-2 branch changes only the MESSAGE. `--count` stays 91.

* **BL, 2026-09-15 — the WORM ledger records host-initiated credential use.** Measured: 60 618 prefixes held 61 111 `execution_complete`, 6 `capability_denied`, 1 approval request and zero credential-use events. Operator decision: all egress, deduped. `TalosContext::record_secret_use` appends `wasi:secret_use` `{surface, key_hash, destination, source, header, actor_id, module_id}` once per distinct `(surface, key hash, destination)` per execution up to `SECRET_USE_LEDGER_CAP` = 64, then one `wasi:secret_use_suppressed`; `resolve_vault_header` takes `(surface, destination)` as REQUIRED parameters and records only on success; `llm_key_with_use_recorded` is ONE home for both `get_llm_api_key` variants. Security decisions: the key is its SHA-256; the destination is the HOST, NATS subject or provider name; nothing records the value. Deliberately recorded at RESOLUTION, not at a confirmed send. Stated limits: the chain path writes no ledger (dormant by config); only first use is recorded, not counts; the env-fallback source is untested. No wire change; workers roll independently.

* **BM, 2026-09-15 — one shared clone-actor service behind MCP `clone_actor` and GraphQL `cloneActor`.** Measured drift: GraphQL skipped the user-ceiling check, the atomic 1 000-actor limit, the secret-grant / budget-policy / approval-policy copies and name validation; MCP cloned a `terminated` (IRREVERSIBLE) source. Latent: 0 clones ever, 10 actors (5 with a budget policy, 1 terminated). Operator decision: ONE implementation `talos_actor_lifecycle_service::clone_actor(pool, repo, CloneActorRequest)` — `user_capability_ceiling` fails CLOSED (unreadable is an error; no row / unrecognised is `http-node`), `check_clone_gates` over `ceiling_permits`; `get_actor_clone_source_scoped` / `insert_actor_clone_scoped` / `ActorCloneSourceRow` DELETED. Behaviour changes, stated: GraphQL gains ceiling refusal, limit, copies, name validation; MCP refuses a terminated source and logs `cloned` on the source. Not changed, recorded: GraphQL `createActor` / `updateActor` keep an inline user-ceiling read (a third copy); the GraphQL source read is no longer in an org-scoped RLS transaction (the read carries `user_id`). `frontend/src/generated/schema.ts` hand-edited; CI's codegen diff is the judge.

* **BN, 2026-09-15 — the never-enforced daily fuel budget is DELETED.** `talos-cost-attribution`'s `get_actor_cost_report` / `check_fuel_budget` (`fuel_budget_daily`, `fuel_alert_threshold_pct`, `alert_triggered`) — measured: no caller; nothing wrote either column; 0 of 5 policies set it. **Correction to package BD**: only the HOURLY gate ever existed (`max_fuel_per_hour`, at `create_execution_under_concurrency_limit`). DELETED: both functions, both columns (migration `20260915120000`). `docs/fuel-budget-sizing.md` names the real bounds — 50 M engine ceiling, step timeout, `max_fuel_per_hour` — and that `max_fuel_per_execution` is stored by `set_actor_budget` but NOT enforced. **Not changed**: the hourly gate, `max_fuel_per_execution`, and siblings `talos_tenancy::TenantIsolation`/`TenantLimits` and `talos-secrets-rotation` — next candidates. A revival fails check 88 (`42703`).

* **BO, 2026-09-15 — `talos-tenancy`'s quota placeholders DELETED.** `TenantLimits` (100 workflows / 50 executions / 100 secrets / 1 000 API calls a minute / 100 000 fuel per execution), `TenantContext`, `TenantIsolation::{validate_access, check_limits}`: zero uses outside the crate; no per-tenant quota is enforced anywhere. Deleted with the crate-wide `#![allow(dead_code)]` (it also silenced the LIVE half — `OrgScope` 7 uses, `TenantReadScope` 45). **Stated limit**: a `pub` item is never reported dead in a library crate — deletion is the guard. **Recorded, not done**: same-shape siblings `talos-secrets-rotation` and `manager.rs`' `VaultSecretProvider` / `AwsSecretProvider` stubs are next.

* **BP, 2026-09-15 — `talos-secrets-rotation`, a never-constructed crate a SOC 2 control cited, DELETED.** 415 lines; MCP-704 removed its only boot binding in May 2026. Real rotation is operator-invoked — `talos_secrets_manager::{rotate_dek, rotate_dek_for_org, rotate_master_key, rotate_secret_value_by_id}`; nothing auto-rotates. SOC 2 CC6.2-07 cited the shim `controller/src/secrets_rotation.rs`; now the real entry points. Deleted with its shim and `mod secrets_rotation;`. Folded: `docs/SECRETS_MANAGEMENT.md` named three nonexistent metrics (`secrets_accessed_total` — a per-`key_path` label this repo forbids —, `secrets_access_denied_total`, `secrets_rotation_age_days`); it now names `talos_secret_decrypt_failures_total`, `talos_dek_cache_size`, `secret_audit_log`, the four live crypto alerts, and states no rotation-age alert exists because nothing computes one. **Not deleted, stated**: `talos-secrets-manager::manager`'s `VaultSecretProvider` / `AwsSecretProvider` stubs keep `allow(dead_code)` — a product direction, not a claimed control. Checks 90 and 91 now skip a listed path missing from disk.

* **BQ, 2026-09-15 — `talos_audit_ledger::verify_execution_chain` (the ONE production verifier call) now runs `verify_chain_anchored`, shipped 2026-09-06 with no production caller; `verify_chain` cannot see a deleted tail.** Measured over the whole `audit-logs` bucket (60 790 prefixes): 60 626 `Anchored`, 163 `MultipleAnchors`, 1 `Unanchored`, 0 empty — all 163 already fail `verify_chain` (duplicate `sequence_num`; pre-AY OAuth-repair re-dispatches, forward-only), so ZERO new failures. HARD fails: `CountMismatch`, `NotTerminal`, `MalformedAnchor`, `MultipleAnchors`; a tail deleted INCLUDING the anchor is `Unanchored`, SOFT by design (indistinguishable from a legacy pre-anchor chain; hard-failing would re-page history). Anchor coverage 12 037/12 038 July, 29 360/29 360 Aug, 19 392/19 392 Sep. `Unanchored` is its own outcome (`JobChainOutcome::Unanchored`, `ChainSweepStats::unanchored`, `unanchored` label on pre-seeded `talos_audit_chain_jobs_swept_total`, a WARN), ABOVE `VerifiedOk`, BELOW `Empty`/`Errored`/`Failed`. **Deliberately NOT a failure; it DOES stamp `talos_audit_chain_last_verified_ok_timestamp_seconds`.** **No new alert**, deliberately (no baseline; `TalosAuditChainJobsUnverifiable`'s unlabelled `sum()` absorbs it). Consumers render `anchor` / `attempt_anchors` beside a possibly-EMPTY `breaks`; `security_audit` states the tail claim in BOTH directions; GraphQL gains `anchor` / `unanchoredAttempts` / `jobsUnanchored`. `unanchored_attempts()` counts from `attempt_anchors`, never the collapsed `anchor` field (FIRST attempt's verdict outside hard failures). `verify_reassembled_chain` extracted so the verifier choice is testable. **No lint**: population ONE call site. No migration, no wire change.

Architecture §6.1, the threat model and SOC 2 CC7.1-01 are pinned to the LIVE catalog by `auditor_doc_claims_tests`; `controller/tests/audit_immutability_tests` pins two catalog invariants (row-guarded ⇒ TRUNCATE-guarded; no immutable table carries an enforced delete action).

* **CH, 2026-09-17 — `admin_event_log` has ONE writer; check 94.** Measured: of FOUR production writers only `talos-actor-repository`'s truncated the summary (1000 bytes, char boundary), bounded `details` (1 MiB) and DLP-redacted; `talos-api-keys` never truncated; `talos-ml`'s lifecycle job and `talos-worker-identity-repository` did neither (model names ARE user content). * **CI, 2026-09-17 — the GitHub webhook dedup window is 24 h and has ONE home.** The GitHub fingerprint is `HMAC(secret, body)`, so a legitimate *Redeliver* and a captured replay are identical — the replay horizon IS the redelivery blackout, a policy dial. Operator decision 2026-09-17: 24 hours. `talos_webhooks::signature::dedup_window(outcome)`: `GITHUB_DEDUP_WINDOW_SECS` 86 400; `DEDUP_WINDOW_SECS` 3 600 for Slack / generic / static-token / open. `WebhookDeduplication` keeps NO window, takes one per call and REFUSES zero. `talos_webhook_duplicate_suppressed_total{format}` (`talos_metrics::WebhookAuthFormat`, 5 pre-seeded) at ONE site `duplicate_suppressed_response`. No alert and deliberately no denominator (the per-`trigger_id` counter deleted 2026-09-11 is not re-admitted). Measured: ONE webhook trigger, ZERO signed deliveries ever — fully LATENT; 239 bytes per claim. Rejected on measurement: freshness from a body timestamp (no field common to GitHub event types; an event parser would become an authorization input). SOC 2 CC6.6-07 now names the format, its absent window and retention. No lint: population ONE dedup call site. No deploy ordering.

* **CK, 2026-09-18 — every execution start runs ONE shared in-transaction actor-budget check.** The five per-actor caps were atomic only in `create_execution_under_concurrency_limit` (one of thirteen INSERT sites). **Live, not latent**: the continuation path ran 3 104 times in 30 days (all `pa-ask-email`, 28 %) never reading per-minute, fuel or tokens; `insert_queued_execution` bound NO actor (rows stamped with the Default actor). **Operator decision 2026-09-18**: one shared check, test runs gated. ONE home `talos_actor_budget_refusal::admit_actor_budget[_for]` (`#[must_use] BudgetAdmission`; roll back THEN record); the enqueue batch is refused WHOLE. `mark_execution_running` → `RetryReset::{Reset, AlreadyRunning, BudgetRefused}`; the batch reports `BatchAdmission::budget_refused`. **Re-attribution measured safe before shipping**: counting the continuation runs against `personal-assistant`, its rolling-1-hour peak over 30 days is 30 against a cap of 40. Chain starts (`insert_chain_execution_row`) check BEFORE dispatch, **not atomically with their row** — stated, 0 chain runs in 30 days. Deleted: `ExecutionRepository::create_execution`, `create_executions_batch_for_workflow`, `WorkflowRepository::create_execution_with_lineage`. **Check 95** (`scripts/lint-execution-start-budget.py`): 13 on main, 0 after, one opt-out on the chain INSERT. **Not changed, stated**: per-workflow concurrency cap, archived gate and pause stay per-path; pre-checks stay fast-fail and own `suspend`.

* **CL, 2026-09-18 — the per-org output sweep and `dekMigrationStatus` cover `workflow_executions_archive`.** Measured: 1 006 archived outputs (v3, 2026-08-05..19) on the global DEK invisible to both, plus 2 565 live v3 outputs crossing the 30-day archive line at ~110 a day. The sweep covers both tiers (`OutputTier::ALL`, live first); the status reports a separate `workflow_executions_archive.output` row under `talos_org_dek_pending`; `OutputReEncryptStats::archive_re_encrypted` names the archived share. Trap: `encrypt_output` resolves the org from the LIVE row, so an archived row would be re-sealed under the global DEK — the sweep seals under the org its page SELECTED via `seal_output`, the one sealing rule. Paged: `OUTPUT_SWEEP_PAGE` = 200, keyset on `id`, an unreadable row counted `failed` and stepped over (was an unbounded `fetch_all`, 23 MB live). Guard `controller/tests/workflow_output_dek_tests` (TC_TESTS). Not changed, stated: `module_executions` has no archive tier; the 2 CJ split-key rows stay recorded; org-less outputs stay on the global DEK by design; the archive's own `org_id` column is not consulted (the workflow join is authoritative).

* **CM, 2026-09-18 — a Google push stream that stops is sayable: `talos_google_push_accepted_total` + `TalosGooglePushSilent`.** Every Google push instrument was a REFUSAL instrument, so a deleted `gmail-push` subscription (3 097 `pa-ask-email` runs / 30 days, ~7/hour) read as a quiet mailbox for four days. `talos_google_push_accepted_total{integration}` (closed `PushIntegration`, pre-seeded) moves once per push when authentication COMPLETES — Gmail in `PubsubJwtVerifier::verify`, GCP after its per-watch service-account check (a verified push for no watch is not accepted) — via `talos_integration_helpers::google_jwt::record_push_accepted`. **`TalosGooglePushSilent`** (warning): `sum by (integration)` 7-day increase > 0 AND 12-hour increase == 0; self-selecting, no watch gauge. The 12 h is DERIVED: over 4 103 pushes, inter-push gap p50 11 min, p99 57 min, longest non-suspend gaps 4.2 h and 3.0 h. `for: 0m` — a `for: 10m` SURVIVED its mutation and was removed. **Stated limit**: after 7 days of silence the look-back empties and the alert clears. **Not changed**: Google Calendar channels (0 here) not covered; a deleted subscription is indistinguishable from an idle mailbox.

* **CN, 2026-09-18 — the two budget pre-checks share one body and one set of count statements.** `ActorRepository::check_execution_allowed` (behind `authorize_workflow_trigger`) put the raw sqlx error in its refusal — handed VERBATIM to MCP (`mcp_denied(-32000, &msg)`) and GraphQL callers — skipped the token cap (all 5 live policies set one), and used bare `.get(…)` (check 55 matches `row.`/`r.` receivers only, so `budget.get` was invisible); every pre-check counted the LIFETIME cap from the live table while the admission counted live + archive. * **CO, 2026-09-18 — `require_2fa` passed on a password alone (`is_2fa_verified = !totp_enabled`; API keys minted true); sessions now record what they PROVED and a privileged tier uses `require_second_factor`.** Measured: 69 mutations in 13 resolver files; 1 user, 0 TOTP enrolled; enrolling revoked nothing (7-day refresh tokens). User decisions 2026-09-18: a privileged tier (not all 69); API keys refused for it. `SessionAuth { PendingSecondFactor, PasswordOnly, SecondFactorVerified }` (`talos-auth-types`) is the one value every mint site passes (`SessionAuth::at_login`, `from_flags`; a contradictory row reads as the more restrictive state); JWT `second_factor_verified` (`#[serde(default)]`, older tokens fail closed); `user_sessions.second_factor_verified` (migration `20260918100000`, NOT NULL DEFAULT false). `require_second_factor` (one home, `talos-api/src/schema/mod.rs`; pure `second_factor_decision`) refuses an API key, a pending or unverified session, and an account no longer enrolled (one PK re-read). Tier is 15: `rotateMasterKey`, `rotateDek`, `rotateEncryptionKey`, `rotateOrgDek`, the six `reEncrypt*` sweeps, `updateAuditSettings`, `createApiKey`, `rotateApiKey`, `registerMcpAgent`, `grantCapabilityCeiling`, `transferOwnership`. **Revocations deliberately keep `require_2fa`** (`revokeApiKey`, `deleteApiKey`, `revokeMcpAgent`, `revokeCapabilityCeiling`) — they reduce privilege and must stay quick. `enableTwoFactor` signs out every session and re-issues the enrolling one verified; a failed revocation logs ERROR under `talos_audit`, does NOT fail the mutation. MCP `grant_capability_ceiling` refuses CROSS-USER grants for every caller, admin included (MCP-1201; self-grant works; 0 MCP grant/revoke calls in 30 days). **Stated limits**: pre-enrolment access tokens stay valid ≤15 min; WebSocket carries no `SecondFactorVerified` (subscriptions only); TOTP is the only second factor (no WebAuthn — SOC 2 gap row unchanged); every existing session reads unverified after deploy, so key rotation, re-encryption sweeps and API-key creation need a 2FA sign-in first.

Now `frontend/src/lib/qrCode.ts::qrCodeImageSrc` builds the data URL; producer test `qr_code_png_is_bare_base64_of_a_png` in `talos-totp-2fa`; GraphQL `qrCodePng` / `qrCodeUrl` describe their contents. Operational note: the screenshot exposed a setup secret; that enrolment was abandoned.

* **CW, 2026-09-20 — a fleet lease for periodic loops; both SLA monitors take it.** Of 67 supervised loops (chart default 2 replicas; all latent), `SlaBreachMonitor` (5 min) and `SlaDegradationMonitor` (15 min) had no claim (0 `workflow_sla_thresholds` rows here). An unreadable lease is NOT a claim (`claim_tick` false, WARN `background_lease_unreadable`). `talos_background_lease_claims_total{task,outcome=claimed|held|error}` seeded for `LEASED_TASKS`; no alert. NOT for concurrent races: a lease is once-per-period, not mutual exclusion.

ONE home `CreateLockMap::acquire_fleet(pool, key, fleet_key)`: local mutex, then BLOCKING `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended($1, 0))` with `lock_timeout = '45s'` (`FLEET_LOCK_TIMEOUT`). * **DC, 2026-09-21 — SIGTERM drains in-flight runs for `RUN_DRAIN_GRACE` = 120 s, then fails ONLY this process's leftovers.** Orphaned runs stayed `running` 60–89 min: 10 rows in 30 days + 2 archived, 7 production (`pa-chief-of-staff` p95 256 s); no owning-controller column, so a boot-time sweep would fail a sibling's runs. `talos_shutdown::inflight`: `InFlightRuns::track(execution_id)` at the THREE chokepoints in `talos-engine/src/nats_run.rs`. On signal `begin_drain()`, `talos_execution_orchestration::shutdown_drain::drain_in_flight_runs` waits up to 120 s (operator decision 2026-09-21), then `talos_execution_finalizer::fail_runs_interrupted_by_shutdown` (own ids only, recorded). Stop timeouts pinned by `every_shipped_stop_timeout_outlasts_the_drain` (grace + 15 s): compose `stop_grace_period: 150s`, chart `controller.terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 150`; worker `40s` / `terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 40`. `RUN_DRAIN_GRACE` is a CONSTANT, deliberately not a knob. **Order is load-bearing**: the RPC-subscriber, background and DLQ stop signals fire AFTER the drain (a draining run still needs the signed-RPC subscribers and the claim responder); the HTTP server is not waited for past the drain (a GraphQL WebSocket holds its connection forever; a sync `call_workflow` is itself a tracked run); the scheduler claims NOTHING while draining. A database unreachable at exit is disclosed (`failed_now: None`, ERROR), never hung or panicked — the stale sweep stays the backstop; the existing `cancel_siblings_on_workflow_fail` trigger closes the module rows. **Stated limits**: crash/SIGKILL still orphans (stale sweep, or opt-in RFC 0003 checkpointing, off); `pa-chief-of-staff`'s p95 exceeds the grace. A full-stack restart also restarts the worker, so a draining run's next node may wait for the new worker; the drain's wake-up is armed before the set is read, a race no test drives. **No lint**: population one.

Now it resolves ids (`resolve_cleanup_ids(conn, …)`, MCP-719 escaping kept) and deletes through `delete_workflows_checked` — ONE guarded statement behind every MCP delete; a parent inside the delete set does not protect its child. Bounded: `CLEANUP_WORKFLOWS_MAX` = 1000 per call with `truncated`; `cleanup_reply` reports `refused_running` / `refused_referenced` as TRUE totals beside lists capped at 50. Behaviour changes, stated: a cleanup leaves behind what the other deletes would refuse. Stated limits: the admin event is still written from a detached task after the delete; the resolved set has no ORDER BY, so which 1000 go first is unspecified; a match whose first 1000 are ALL refused makes no progress. SURVIVOR closed: dropping `user_id = $1` passed because the `workflows` RLS policy hid the other tenant anyway; closed by an UNSCOPED-connection test.

Series: `talos_auth_token_reuse_total{outcome}` (five values, pre-seeded) and `talos_auth_rotation_audit_arm_total{outcome}`. The caller-facing answer is deliberately unchanged (`Invalid or expired refresh token`; a split is an oracle); `detector_unreadable` fails the REQUEST closed, fail-open on the RESPONSE only. Alerts: `TalosAuthRefreshTokenReuseDetected` `> 0` over 15 m, `critical`, selecting `detected` AND `revoke_failed`; `TalosAuthRotationAuditArmFailing` ratio > 50 % with a floor of 5 over 24 h (rotations run 8–50/day, busiest hour 6, some days none). Deliberately NO alert on `detector_unreadable` (no reachable floor; the series come first). Zero added database work.

* **DK, 2026-09-22 — a model read that did not ANSWER is no longer "Model not found"; `classify_model_lookup` is ONE home.** SIX `let Ok(Some(m)) = … else` collapses in `talos-mcp-handlers/src/ml.rs` (four mutating); `talos-ml`'s `correction.rs` / `teacher_audit.rs` had the same `_ => return Err(…NotFound)` collapse; `provision.rs:202`'s wildcard is CORRECT and must not be "fixed". `classify_model_lookup -> Result<T, ModelLookupRefusal>`; all seven handlers route through it; both belts read the three-valued `lookup_dataset_tenancy`. Absent and foreign stay ONE answer (no id oracle). **Deliberately NOT re-litigated**: `NotFound` vs `Denied` for that answer. **Deliberately NOT changed**: `serve.rs`'s two belts (both arms → `NotAvailable`, documented degrade-to-LLM). **Behaviour changes, instrument-side, stated**: absent moves from `error` (Finding) to `not_found` (Declined), as do the typed `ServeError::NotFound` / `TeacherAuditError::NotFound` arms; unreadable is `failed`, disclaiming absence; wire bytes unchanged. **MEASURED SURVIVOR**: removing the correction belt's `t.user_id == user_id` guard changes nothing observable (measured) — a downstream refusal holds.

* **DL, 2026-09-22 — SQL-shaped lint checks read statements through ONE statement-aware lexer.** Check 39 matched 0 lines while 19 wrapped statements existed; the `talos-execution-finalizer` source pins matched only their own `let needle` line. ONE home `scripts/lint_lib/ruststmt.py`; `strip_test_modules` blanks a column-0 `#[cfg(test)]` only when the NEXT line opens a `mod` — deliberately conservative. Checks 39 and 46 re-pointed, both at ZERO (39 now examines 19 statements; 46 one, finding nothing new — a gate improvement, stated, not a bug fix). Sub-leg 46b gains leg (b): one home for the finalizer statements, keyed on the GUARD `NOT IN ('completed', 'failed', 'cancelled', 'resuming')`. Fixed: `fail_execution_unless_terminal`'s no-`completed_at` variant moved into the leaf as `fail_workflow_execution_unless_terminal_without_completed_at`. **Deliberately NOT re-pointed**: check 86 (11 vs 4, the 7 added all legitimate — 7 markers on correct code), check 12 (18 vs 2, 15 correct), 46b's whole-file joiner. **Recorded, not fixed**: `talos-actor-lifecycle-service/src/clone.rs:267`'s `Option<i64>` min-only clamp; check 42's 16-line window, too short for 3 of 16 sites. **Behaviour change, stated**: both checks look 8 lines above for the opt-out marker, not 4. `--count` stays **95**.

* **DM, 2026-09-22 — advisory-DB age: one home, three gauges, three alerts.** `check_advisory_db_age` warns at 30 days and in production REFUSES compiles past `TALOS_ADVISORY_DB_MAX_AGE_DAYS` (default 90); measured 75 of 90 here; controller (2026-07-09) and builder (2026-07-07) images bake SEPARATE copies. ONE home `talos_compilation::advisory_db`: `Result<u64, AdvisoryDbAgeError>` (`Unreadable`/`FutureDated`, never `0`), pure `advisory_db_gate_outcome(age, max, production)`. Hourly `BackgroundTask::AdvisoryDbAgeGauge` sets `talos_advisory_db_age_days`, `talos_advisory_db_max_age_days`, `talos_advisory_db_age_enforced` by `copy` — ABSENT until sampled; `talos_advisory_db_age_samples_total{copy,outcome}` pre-seeded. Alerts: `TalosAdvisoryDbAging` (warning), `TalosAdvisoryDbExpired` (critical, `age >= talos_advisory_db_max_age_days and enforced == 1`), `TalosAdvisoryDbUnreadable` (warning, 2 samples). **Stated limit**: builder copy unsampled (`copy="controller"` only). **Folded**: checks 89–95's `OUT="$(python3 …)"` capture under `set -e` aborted the lint on any finding before printing it; fixed, six sites. Check 89 limits: untracked files and `const`-routed names invisible.

* **DN, 2026-09-22 — the whole-codebase-review section compressed to decisions, with the losslessness checker finally wired as a gate.** Measured before designing: CLAUDE.md was 752 579 bytes (~188k tokens at every session start and every agent brief); the review section was 364 801 bytes (48.5 % of the file) in 188 lines, and 103 of its 130 bullets — the per-package entries from package C to DM, median 3 065 bytes, max 6 173 — were the mass; the 27 short "Decisions" bullets from the original review were already digest-shaped and stay. All 103 post-dated `check-engineering-log.py`'s single `BASE_REV`, so the first split's proof did not constrain them; and that script was wired into NOTHING — no lint check, no CI job, no hook — so the first split's proof had only ever run by hand. Now: the 103 bullets are rewritten to decisions only (each keeps every DECIDED / deliberately NOT / REJECTED / measured population / latent claim / stated limit / one-home identifier / migration id / check number, drops the discovery and mutation narrative) and moved VERBATIM, contiguous, to `docs/engineering-log/2026-09-10-whole-codebase-review.md`; the section is 128 632 bytes and the file 516 410 bytes (**31.4 %** in BYTES — the last split was 71 % by lines and 44 % by bytes, so bytes is the number). The checker has ONE `check()` body over a list of `BASES` (both splits' pre-split commits, FULL 40-char ids because GitHub serves fetch-by-SHA only for the full id), a `--self-test` of seven fixtures each flipping exactly one leg (its first draft doubled as a removed line and failed two legs; the marker must sit in the story's INTERIOR or the ±2-line context reads the digest's own heading), on-demand fetch of a base missing from a shallow checkout (proved from a depth-1 HTTPS clone of GitHub), and unreadable-is-FAIL. **Check 96** runs both; `--count` moves to 96. **Stated limits, the checker's own**: leg 2's digest representation is a token-overlap heuristic — a compact bullet that drops a decision while keeping one distinctive identifier passes it; the human review of each compact bullet against its source is the rest of the guard, and the verbatim archive is what makes a dropped clause recoverable. Deliberately NOT touched: the lint-check list (190 KB, 25 % of the file) — its inline documentation IS each check's specification, and the digest's own rule says so; and the per-class digests above the review section, already split on 2026-09-09.

* **DO, 2026-09-22 — CI's clippy covers every target; 107 test-target warning sites in 55 files burned to zero.** `--all-targets` now in `quality.yml`, check 7 and the pre-push comment; `--no-deps` KEPT (dependencies' lints are not ours). The lib-only gate had never seen these, which is why packages AO, AP and DL each found this class by hand. **Two were semantic**: `controller/tests/execution_metrics_tests.rs` ignored the `#[must_use] BudgetAdmission` its fixture returns since CK, so a refused admission would have failed every downstream assertion against a row that did not exist; `talos-memory-ranking`'s `prov_days` had zero callers from the commit that introduced it (#791) and is DELETED. **Fix classes, decided**: a `std::sync::Mutex` guard held across `.await` becomes `tokio::sync::Mutex` at an IDENTICAL guard scope; a constant assertion becomes the compile-time pin `const _: () = assert!(…)`, stronger than the test it replaces; large test futures are `Box::pin`ned; float equality is an epsilon, except bit-exact round-trips (`to_bits()`) and `NEG_INFINITY`, asserted by its two defining predicates because an epsilon against it is NaN. **Seven `#[allow]`s, each with a written reason and none a suppression of a defect**, one of them `items_after_test_module` in `crash_recovery.rs` because check 58's over-strip tripwire pins production code sitting AFTER a test module. **One suggestion REFUSED**: `cmp_owned` on `serde_json::Value` vs re-serialised text changes the test's meaning. **Stated**: `talos-workflow-job-protocol`'s `test_support.rs` counts in CI only because `talos-memory`'s dev-dependency enables its feature. **Not changed**: the `clippy::pedantic` posture of the engine and protocol crates.

* **DP, 2026-09-22 — the frontend's session refresh has ONE home.** Survey first, and it found nothing else: `dist` 1.9 MB / 67 hashed assets, first load ≈200 KB gzipped, production CSP without `unsafe-inline`, HSTS, COOP/CORP, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, no tokens in web storage, no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, no `eval`, pollers 60/30/10 s with background refetch off. **The finding**: `refreshToken` existed in THREE copies (`graphqlClient.ts`, `authedFetch.ts`, `auth.ts`), two with their own in-flight deduper and the 14-minute timer with none, so a GraphQL auth error, a REST 401 and the timer could refresh CONCURRENTLY; refresh tokens rotate, so the loser sends a rotated token, the detector answers `within_grace`, and the user sees an auth error. Measured in `rotated_session_audit`: **95 rotation pairs, 2 of them 0.4 s apart**; the copies had already drifted once (the REST seed hit `/graphql`, a 405 in production). ONE home `frontend/src/lib/session.ts`: `refreshSession()` (one in-flight promise, one mutation text selecting every field any caller reads, `refreshed: false` the ONLY failure shape — the refresh token is an HttpOnly cookie no caller can inspect, so a finer split is unactionable), `seedCsrfCookie`/`ensureCsrfCookie`, `isAuthErrorMessage` listing the three backend phrasings once; imports only `config` and `csrf` so it cannot cycle. Guard: `session.test.ts` drives the three PRODUCTION surfaces concurrently and asserts exactly one mutation — red on the pre-fix tree by construction. **Recorded, not changed**: an anonymous load issues one doomed `refreshToken` (no cookie, no audit row); `me` fires twice in dev (StrictMode). **Not measured**: the authenticated dashboard's waterfall.
* **DQ, 2026-09-22 — check 42 reads the statement, not a 16-line window.** The org-pinned-table create rule (`INSERT INTO workflows|actors|secrets` must run on a tenant-scoped executor, RFC 0006) was a grep that looked 16 lines below each `INSERT` line for one of five executor spellings. Measured statement-aware on `ecf5fe4c`: **15** such statements (a line grep said 16), all 15 on a `&mut *tx` / `conn` executor — 0 findings, correctly — but the executor of THREE sits 18–22 lines below the literal (`talos-secrets-manager/src/manager.rs:1415` and `:3710`, `talos-workflow-repository/src/workflows.rs:739`), past the window, and `&*self.pool` / `self.pool()` / `&state.db_pool` were spellings the regex never knew. Mutation-proved on the real tree: `.fetch_one(&mut *tx)` → `.fetch_one(&self.db_pool)` at manager.rs's far site is reported by the new check and invisible to the old window. Now `scripts/lint-org-write-executor.py` (on the DL lexer, one `scan_source` body for the run and its 9-case self-test): every org-table `INSERT` literal is followed to the FIRST executor call within 60 lines and the ARGUMENT is classified by SHAPE — names `pool` / `db_pool` / `db` with no `mut` is the bare pool; anything else is scoped — and a statement with no executor in range is a `no-executor` finding, not a pass. Fails loudly (exit 2) at zero matches; opt-out within **8** lines above (was 4, DL's rule). `--count` stays **96**. **Stated limit**: the executor is found by forward scan from the literal's line, so a literal bound to a local and executed in a later statement attributes the next executor it meets — loud direction.
* **DS, 2026-09-22 — a request already on the wire when a refresh settles retries with the fresh cookie instead of refreshing again.** DP's in-flight promise covers callers whose auth failures OVERLAP, not one whose 401 arrives AFTER the refresh settled: measured on the first dashboard load after DP, two rotations **0.4 s apart**, both successful, `within_grace` 0 — chained, not raced, and the second redundant (3 such pairs in 7 days; the two pre-DP ones were races). `session.ts` keeps a refresh EPOCH that advances **on SUCCESS only**; each wrapper captures `currentRefreshEpoch()` before sending and recovers through `recoverSession(epochAtSend)`, which answers "already fresh" with no network call; the two WebSocket sites recover from their connect-time epoch. The decision is the pure `refreshSucceededSince`; `isRetry` still bounds recovery to ONE attempt per request. **Found by the new tests, same class**: `authedFetch`'s 401 arm and its auth-message-body arm were independent, so a 401 whose body also read "Not authenticated" refreshed TWICE when the first refresh failed — now one attempt (`recoveryTried`). **A failed refresh never advances the epoch** (a later 401 must not trust a cookie that is not fresh), pinned. **Stated limit**: the WebSocket half is a TEXTUAL pin. **Recorded, not changed**: the anonymous load's doomed refresh; `me` twice in dev.
* **DT, 2026-09-22 — 2FA login had never worked: the frontend's `verifyTwoFactor` document named an input type the schema does not have.** `auth.ts` wrote `$input: VerifyTwoFactorInput!`; the schema has called it `Verify2FAInput` since at least 2026-05-18 (both spellings present at `8f13f1e9`; #519 rewrote the string and kept the wrong name). Every 2FA login was refused by schema validation before reaching the verifier. **Latent four months and found on first use** — no account had TOTP enrolled until the operator enrolled after CP fixed the QR; the first password login after that produced 8 `login_success` rows, 8 pending sessions and **0** `talos_auth_2fa_attempts_total` in either outcome. **Why nothing saw it**: codegen plucks `gql`-tagged documents and `.graphql` files and CI validates those; a document in a BARE template literal is invisible, and `auth.ts` and `session.ts` are all bare, while `AuthForm.test.tsx` mocks the mutation. `inline_documents.test.ts` now DERIVES the population — every bare `query|mutation|subscription` literal under `src/`, comments stripped — and validates each against `schema.graphql`, with a floor of 40 on the measured **53** so a shrunken scan fails rather than passes. **Measured: 53 bare documents, exactly ONE invalid.** **Deliberately NOT done**: converting the six to `gql` tags — that routes them through codegen and regenerates `graphql.ts`; the test covers the class, tags would cover the six. **Recorded**: the 8 pending sessions expire on their own; no account state changed.
* **DU, 2026-09-22 — the WebSocket lane has series: `talos_ws_handshakes_total{outcome}`, `talos_ws_session_ends_total{reason}`, `talos_ws_operations_total{outcome}`, `talos_ws_active_sessions`.** `/ws` is the fourth bearer surface (the access-token COOKIE on the upgrade) and was the last with no counter: nine refusal/close arms — missing, malformed and disallowed Origin (the Cross-Site WebSocket Hijacking signal), no/invalid cookie, a non-UUID `sub`, a first frame that is not `connection_init`, no `connection_init` within 30 s, the expiry close — every one a log line only. AX's class one surface over. `classify_origin`/`classify_auth` are PURE (the verifier a closure, so `AuthService` is not needed); `report_handshake` is the ONE site that counts and logs; the caller-facing `connection_error` stays ONE sentence for all three token outcomes (an oracle otherwise); the auth verdict is HELD until `connection_init` so a cookieless client that never sends one ends as `init_not_received`; `WsActiveSession` is a Drop guard so the gauge releases however a session ends. Three closed enums in `talos_metrics::security`, every counter value pre-seeded (15 lines); the gauge is not. **No alert, deliberately**: no baseline. **Measured**: one dashboard load authenticates 3 sockets in 400 ms — one per subscription, DV's population. **Stated limit**: no test drives a real socket through `handle_websocket_auth` (no WebSocket harness in the workspace); the live read after deploy is the wiring's proof.
* **DW, 2026-09-22 — an anonymous page load no longer spends two round trips learning it is anonymous.** Both auth cookies are HttpOnly (correctly), so the SPA could not tell "no session" from "session with an expired access token": every anonymous load asked `me`, then issued a `refreshToken` refused with `No refresh token found in cookies` — a refusal that writes no audit row and no log line (113 successful refreshes in 7 days, **0** recorded failures, so the waste was invisible server-side by construction). `set_session_cookies` now sets a third cookie `talos_session_present=1` (`SESSION_PRESENT_COOKIE`): deliberately NOT HttpOnly, its only content its existence, Secure/SameSite=Strict/Path=`/`, and the refresh cookie's lifetime exactly (ONE `REFRESH_COOKIE_TTL`) so it never claims a session that could not be refreshed; `clear_session_cookies` removes it. `AuthContext` renders anonymous with ZERO requests when the marker is absent; **it gates only the SPECULATIVE probe** — `recoverSession` is unchanged, so a wrong marker costs nothing new. `readCookie(name)` in `csrf.ts` is the ONE cookie reader. **Deploy seam, stated**: sessions minted before this deploy carry no marker, so every existing browser session reads anonymous until the user logs in once. Guards: HttpOnly must be FALSE, `max_age` equals the refresh cookie's, and the HttpOnly loop excludes the marker BY NAME so a fourth token cookie still fails. **Measured value on this fleet, stated plainly**: near zero — the operator's loads are never anonymous — the class is "a request whose failure is known before it is sent".
* **DV, 2026-09-22 — every GraphQL subscription rides ONE socket per page; the server lane multiplexes by id.** `createSubscription` opened its OWN `WebSocket` per call (8 caller files), each with its own handshake, backoff and expiry close; and the SERVER served one subscription per socket BY CONSTRUCTION — `handle_graphql_ws` awaited the response stream inline, so a second `start` was not read until the first ended, and `stop` echoed `complete` while the stream ran on. The session is now generic over `Stream + Sink`: one writer task over a bounded `OUTBOUND_FRAME_BUFFER` (256) channel, one task per `start`, `stop` ABORTS it, `SessionTasks`' `Drop` aborts every task with the socket (the expiry deadline DROPS the session future, so a guard and not a code path stops orphans), `reap()` frees completed ids, and two refusals join the closed `WsOperationOutcome`: `refused_too_many` at `MAX_SUBSCRIPTIONS_PER_SOCKET` = 16 live, and `refused_duplicate_id`; all five pre-seeded, and `started` counts ACCEPTED starts only. Client hub `frontend/src/lib/wsHub.ts` owns the lazy socket, page-unique ids, replay of every live `start` after each `connection_ack`, idle close, the 24 h lifetime, and ONE auth-recovery site through `recoverSession(this.epochAtConnect)` (DS's rule at the connection). **Behaviour changes, stated**: `stop` now ends the server stream; a 17th live subscription is refused with an `error` frame the hub logs once and does not retry (the compare page holds one per compared actor — 10 actors here); `talos_ws_active_sessions` now reads ≈ open pages, not open subscriptions. **Stated limits**: no browser is driven against the real server; the hub is per TAB; the cap is a CONSTANT, deliberately not a knob; the client has no series. **Three SURVIVORS closed**: a deleted `reap`, `started` recorded before the gates, and data fanned to every handler.

* **DX, 2026-09-23 — a job that was never executed is no longer judged as a module error.** Measured over 30 days (live + archive): of 104 workflow failures, **11** are `Job dispatch` refused on AGE (the module did NOT run — all 11 module rows consumed ZERO fuel) and **2** are `no responders`; the 1 `Job result` stale case must STAY refused (the module DID run). **The worker already stamped a typed token and the controller threw it away** — `signature_failure_payload` publishes `reason_class`, the dispatcher read only `output_payload["error"]` and handed the prose to a Gmail-authored `retry_condition` (10 of 11) whose answer was no; `talos-retry-intelligence` has ZERO occurrences of `liveness`/`freshness`/`stale_timestamp`, so its fallback is `unknown` = non-transient. ONE home in `talos-workflow-job-protocol`: `verify_failure_class` MOVED from the worker's private copy (the move makes the match **exhaustive**, so a new variant fails to compile instead of silently reading as tampering), the CLOSED `PRE_EXECUTION_REJECTION_CLASSES` = `{stale_timestamp}` — membership needs **safe** AND **useful**, so `replay` is excluded because the receiver has SEEN that nonce, `mismatch` because re-sending re-admits a forgery, `key_config`/`scheme_mismatch` because identical bytes earn an identical refusal — and `is_pre_execution_rejection` = `status == Failed` AND that class. **The status test is load-bearing**: a module's JSON reaches the wire only under `Success`, so without it a module could return `{"success": false, "reason_class": "stale_timestamp"}` and re-run its own completed side effect. The 503 is classified by TYPE (`NoRespondersError`), never by prose. `LIVENESS_REDISPATCH_MAX` = **3**, a CONSTANT deliberately INDEPENDENT of `max_retries`; backoff DERIVED per cause (1 s stale, 10 s no-responders → ≈70 s). **The attempt window is NOT re-derived** — `clamp_attempt_timeout` still owns it, pinned. **NO metric, a measurement not an omission**: this crate reaches `talos-metrics` only transitively, so a series costs a dependency edge the attempt-window package already declined; the record is an `execution_events` `node_retrying` row with `error_class = "liveness:<cause>"` — queryable, and stated plainly as NOT alertable. Re-dispatch logs INFO, only EXHAUSTION at WARN (check 69). **This package's own first draft carried the 2026-09-14 OAuth-repair defect** — two branches stamped index 1 twice, i.e. two chains in one audit partition; `next_dispatch_attempt` is now one formula, pinned. **Stated limits**: no test drives a real worker over real NATS; the PIPELINE path is unchanged and dormant by config, recorded not done; the predicate proves the RECEIVER's claim and a fleet-key holder could lie — the bound is what makes that uninteresting.

* **DY, 2026-09-23 — the GraphQL privileged gate was the last bearer surface with no series.** The survey REFUTED its own lead twice: one ML kNN statement at **40% of all DB time** is 216 s of a 538 s / 143.4 h total = **0.104% of one core** (a share is not a cost — read the denominator); and the 30-day failure population is EXHAUSTED as a source of platform work — of 105 failures, 30 suspend-shaped with 721–8 567 s spans against 180–300 s budgets, 38 dns declined 2026-09-21, 15 liveness fixed by DX, 22 long tail: **79% one external root cause**, a host that sleeps. **The gap**: `require_second_factor` guards **17 call sites / 15 mutations** with 4 refusal reasons and `require_platform_admin` 12 more, both with zero metric references, while every other bearer surface had one. Now `talos_privileged_op_total{outcome}` (7 values) and `talos_platform_admin_checks_total` (4), closed enums, ALL pre-seeded, ONE recording site per gate; `RateLimitKind` 5 → 7. **`permitted` is counted, not only refusals** — a refusal count with no denominator cannot tell a deployment nobody has been refused on from one whose gate is not wired. **Three NON-policy outcomes stay distinct**: `unauthenticated`, and `unreadable` (the rule could not be READ — #757's precedent; REFUSES, never grants). **The metric label and the caller-facing `reason` are pinned EQUAL** across two crates. **TWO throttle kinds, not one** — an operator raising a limit needs to know which. **The `talos-metrics` edge is taken here and DX's refusal does NOT transfer**, checked: already in `talos-api`'s graph (26 transitive paths), so no new compilation unit and no cycle. **NO alert**: no baseline. **The MEASURED SURVIVOR is the package's own central claim** — narrowing the recorder to `if !outcome.permitted()` passed the ENTIRE talos-api suite, because all five outcomes reachable without a database are refusals; closed by `controller/tests/privileged_gate_permitted_tests` (CTRL_TESTS) driving the real gate against a real `AuthService` and `users` row. **Interleaving lesson**: a per-module lock serialises a module against itself and not against the binary, so the talos-api metrics lock has ONE home (`METRICS_SERIES_LOCK`). **Stated limits**: `permitted`/`not_enrolled` run only in the DB binary; nothing alerts; the other fail-open-shaped gates (capability ceilings, module rate limits, Rhai policy) are unmeasured and NOT claimed covered.

* **DZ, 2026-09-23 — caller-supplied module source was silently rewritten, and only on two of six paths.** An HTML escaper written the obvious way (`'&' => out.push_str("&amp;")`) came back from `compile_custom_sandbox` with its literals decoded to the bare characters. **The rewritten form is still valid Rust**, so it compiles and every does-it-build check passes while the escaper has become a NO-OP emitting the character it exists to neutralise; `&#39;` is the only member whose rewrite tends to break the build, which is luck and not a guard. **Population: SIX handlers take caller source and exactly TWO decoded**, so identical source produced DIFFERENT modules depending on which tool compiled it, and the documented pair `lint_sandbox`/`compile_custom_sandbox` disagreed; the chain existed in THREE copies. **The repair is KEPT** (`HashMap&lt;K, V&gt;` from a client that misreads `serde_json`'s `<` escapes is not valid Rust) but is now **literal-aware with ONE home**: `talos_compilation::source_entities::decode_entities_outside_literals` decodes in CODE regions only, never inside a string literal, char literal or comment — and outside those an HTML entity is not valid Rust anyway, so a decode there can only repair client damage. Char literals are tracked for ONE reason: a `'"'` literal would otherwise open a phantom string and desynchronise the file. **The repair is now REPORTED** (`SourceDecode::note`) in both directions, including the count left alone inside literals. **Second defect, same surface**: `hot_update_module`'s `rust_code` is OPTIONAL and omitting it recompiles the STORED source, minting a fresh `content_hash` — the shape of a real replacement; the reply now carries `source: replaced | recompiled_stored` from the pure `hot_update_source_disposition`, and the note says the fresh hash is NOT evidence the caller's source was applied. **M6 SURVIVED and refuted this package's own comment**: reordering the entity table changes nothing, because no member is a prefix of another — the "longest-first" claim was unprovable; prefix-freeness is the property that IS load-bearing and is pinned. **Deliberately NOT changed**: `talos_text_util::decode_html_entities` keeps its literal-blind chain and its four `talos-engine` callers, because a RHAI condition is a different language and a different question; same blind spot, recorded not fixed. **No lint check was added**, `--count` stays **96**: six call sites in three files, below #765's bar. **Unrelated pre-existing flake fixed and called out**: DM's advisory-DB test failed ~1 run in 3 because `File::open(dir).set_modified()` returns `Ok` on macOS without always taking effect; it now retries and VERIFIES through `advisory_db_age_days` before asserting.

* **EA, 2026-09-23 — two instruments that overstated what they observed, and one claimed defect WITHDRAWN on measurement.** **WITHDRAWN — `applied_max_fuel` is not a defect**: a 2026-07-26 change had already added `node_max_fuel_override` and `configured_max_fuel` beside it with a comment naming exactly this confusion, and `effective_max_fuel` was renamed on 2026-09-03 because the old name over-claimed. All three were in the responses that misled me; I read the first and stopped. Nothing shipped — recorded so the next session does not "fix" a field that already carries its own disclosure. **(1) `ws_init_not_received` fired at WARN on ordinary browser behaviour**: the pre-init loop collapsed two endings by choice, so a client that CLOSED before `connection_init` and one that held the socket for the full 30 s deadline rendered identically, under a message claiming the deadline had elapsed — measured, it was the controller's ONLY WARN, logged **535 microseconds** after that socket authenticated. `ClosedBeforeInit` (**DEBUG** — check 69 / #787) is now distinct from `InitNotReceived`, which NARROWS to what its name always said; `ALL` is 10, all pre-seeded, and nothing alerts on either so the narrowing's blast radius is a dashboard that does not exist. **(2) `talos_platform_admin_checks_total{outcome="unauthenticated"}` is STRUCTURALLY UNREACHABLE**, measured live: an unauthenticated call moved it by ZERO, because `require_scope(Admin)` refuses first and sits above **all 11** `require_platform_admin` call sites — while **0 of 16** `require_second_factor` sites have one, which is why ITS `unauthenticated` is reachable and was observed moving. **DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED BY REORDERING THE GATES**: both orders refuse and only the caller-facing sentence differs, so reordering 11 security-sensitive call sites to make a counter movable is the tail wagging the dog. The variant STAYS on the enum (the evaluator must be total) and stays SEEDED (a future call site ahead of the scope gate would otherwise be born at 1 and read 0 forever under `increase()`); the HELP text now says it reads 0 on every deployment, why, and which counter to read instead. **N3 SURVIVED**: reverting the `Message::Close` arm restores the defect and the pure mapping's own test structurally cannot see it (checks 74b/79b); closed by a TEXTUAL pin. **Stated limit**: the pre-init loop is still not DRIVEN — that needs `handle_websocket_auth` made generic over its socket as DV did for the session. **No lint check was added**, `--count` stays **96**. **Unrelated pre-existing flake fixed and called out**: CV's relay test failed CI at 16 of 50 rows — `drain` waits out 400 ms of quiet on the BROADCAST channel while the assertion counts rows written by the PERSIST subscription, so one going quiet says nothing about the other's INSERTs; `rows_until` polls both pools to a 10 s deadline, NO assertion changed, and the message is now DIAGNOSTIC because the slow-consumer hypothesis could not be ruled out from the CI log.

* **EB, 2026-09-23 — the "flaky integration test class" was measured and is NOT a class; one home for the bounded wait instead.** CI ground truth, `quality.yml` last **40 runs**: 32 success, 5 cancelled, **2 failure — and BOTH the same test** (CV's relay test). The other flake never failed in CI at all; they do not even share an environment. **The obvious detector was BUILT and REJECTED**: `sleep(...)` within 12 lines of an `assert` outside a bounded loop, over 187 test files → 20 reported, of which **6 false positives** (the sleep sits inside a `for`-bounded poll; the loop check knew `while`/`loop` and not `for`), **5 negative controls** asserting an absence (which fail in the SAFE direction), **5 legitimate** and **3 genuine latent risks** — **15% precision, and 0-for-2 against the flakes that actually happened**, since one failing wait is a `timeout`-based drain and the other a filesystem mtime. Green over both defects it was written for is #624 / checks 64-65's shape; `--count` stays **96**. **The finding that IS actionable**: `eventually(what, probe)` existed in **exactly 1 of 187 test files**, as a PRIVATE fn in the SIBLING file of the one that flaked — same package, same two-replica NATS pattern, written days apart. **Privacy was the cause — there was nothing to find.** `common::eventually`/`eventually_default` now live in the shared harness. **NONE of the 3 latent sites is adopted, each reason measured**: one counts statements on a THREAD-LOCAL counter a polling probe would itself increment; one asserts a COLD registry after the wait, so a probe RPC would move `base_ok` off 0; one is in a crate with no shared harness and one site does not justify a test-utils crate. **P2 SURVIVED and then HUNG**: deleting the deadline arm loops forever, and a `#[should_panic]` guard never returns — a hung binary burns the CI timeout and reports nothing; the guard now bounds the JOIN from outside. **Harness lesson**: the run hit its own timeout, the exception propagated past the revert, and the mutation was left in the tree; the harness now reverts in a `finally`. **Stated limit**: the second guard is a TEXTUAL pin that sees a locally-DEFINED helper, not a hand-rolled poll under another name.

* **EC, 2026-09-23 — two tools rejected the name they had just handed the caller back.** Measured over the 338 declared input properties: `timeout_secs` is the spelling **17** tools declare and **33** response keys render, and `set_workflow_execution_timeout` is the ONE tool declaring `timeout_seconds` — while the field renders back as `execution_timeout_secs`, so one tool spells one concept THREE ways and reading the value back to reuse its name is the one move it refuses. Separately `create_schedule` requires `cron_expression` while the readers render `cron`. **Population is exactly TWO and that is the whole class** — the duration-family sweep found no third. **Aliasing, not renaming**, because a rename breaks callers that work today; canonical names unchanged, and the CANONICAL WINS when both are supplied rather than a coin-flip. Already the house answer at **15** ad-hoc `or_else(|| args.get(…))` sites, so this gives that convention ONE home: `talos_mcp_handlers::utils::arg_or_alias`. **An explicit `null` falls THROUGH to the alias** — otherwise `{"timeout_seconds": null, "timeout_secs": 900}` reads `null` and never reaches the alias, making it unreachable exactly when needed. **Q5 SURVIVED**: reverting a call site to a bare `args.get(canonical)` leaves the helper's tests green while the advertised alias silently stops working (checks 74b/79b); closed by a pin asserting BOTH halves — the alias is DECLARED (an accepted-but-undeclared alias is undiscoverable) and READ through `arg_or_alias` (a declared-but-unread one fails at call time). **No lint check was added**, `--count` stays **96**: population two. **One BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, stated**: `unknown_argument_warning` no longer answers `cron` with "did you mean 'cron_expression'", because warning about an argument that works sends the caller to fix a non-problem; its test was rewritten to pin the new contract, with a CONTROL so it cannot pass because the suggester broke entirely.

* **ED, 2026-09-23 — a Plaid integration, and the SHAPE was decided by an existing control rather than chosen.** Plaid takes its `access_token` in the JSON REQUEST BODY, and at the time a module could not put a secret there — `vault://` resolved into HEADERS only, `get_secret` hands the guest an opaque `u64`, `expose_secret` is hardcoded `false` at every dispatch path — so `talos-plaid` is a CONTROLLER-side crate. **[The "widening `vault://` to bodies would be a security regression" half of that reasoning was WITHDRAWN by EE: placement is not disclosure. The crate's SCOPE became wrong rather than the crate itself.]** **It also does not fit the OAuth toolkit**: `link_token` → BROWSER → `public_token` → server exchange → long-lived `access_token`, with no authorize redirect, no state token and no refresh token, so `OAuthIntegration`/`consume_oauth_state` do not apply to ACQUISITION; `OAuthCredentialService` remains the right STORE, for its tenancy gating. **Posture, stricter than the work actor's**: `max_llm_tier = tier1` **plus** `egress_scope = public` — tier-1 structurally bars every external LLM provider so transaction data cannot reach one, while public egress still permits the HTTPS call. **Decisions**: `PlaidEnv` is a CLOSED enum with **no `Default`** (sandbox would silently read nothing in production; production would point a real credential at real banks over a typo) and `development` is rejected outright; all three env vars unset = OFF, a PARTIAL or unrecognised configuration is a hard error naming the variable WITHOUT echoing values, empty counts as absent (check 73); redacting `Debug` on every credential-bearing type (lint 37); bodies through `read_json_capped` (lint 31); the error path parses `error_type`/`error_code` and DISCARDS the raw body, which can echo request fields. **The `/transactions/sync` loop has TWO independent stops**: a 40-page cap (20 000 transactions) AND a cursor-did-not-advance check — without the second, a provider that keeps saying `has_more` while returning the same cursor burns all 40 pages every call. When the cap binds the caller is TOLD (`SyncPage::truncated`), because a prefix presented as the whole week is the misleading-report class applied to someone's money. `Balances` are `Option` and an unreadable balance renders UNKNOWN, never `0.00`. Plaid signs OUTflows POSITIVE — inverting that turns a spending report into an income report. **Stated limits**: nothing is driven against the real Plaid API and the honest guard is the first sandbox call; the browser Link step is NOT built, so the only source of a `public_token` is Plaid Sandbox; no workflow consumes this yet.

* **EE, 2026-09-23 — `vault://` substitution reached headers only, so an API that takes its credential in the JSON BODY was unbuildable as a module. The claim that widening it would be a security regression is WITHDRAWN — it conflated PLACEMENT with DISCLOSURE.** Measured: `resolve_vault_header` is called from **six** host surfaces and every one substituted into a HEADER; `expose_secret` is the literal `false` at **all five** non-test dispatch sites; **0 of the 49** catalog templates referencing `vault://` place a credential in a body — an impossibility, not a preference. Twenty-four hours earlier this session had called that widening a **security regression**; the correction is the finding: the property the three controls defend is **use without disclosure** — the guest must never HOLD the plaintext — and where the host puts it on the wire is orthogonal. **The guest keeps the PLACEHOLDER**: resolved bytes are built after the headers and handed straight to `reqwest`, never returned, logged or stored, so what reaches `module_executions.output_data` is `vault://plaid/…`. `VaultPlacement::{Header, JsonBody}` makes placement a PARAMETER of the one resolver, so the allowlist grant, the reserved-host deny list, the tier-1 ceiling and the WORM ledger entry are the SAME code for both. **Four rules, each because a body is not a header**: JSON only, so a non-JSON content type is REFUSED rather than rewritten (the type read from the guest's own headers, never assumed); string VALUES only, never keys (a key is structure — substituting there could collide two fields or invent one); substitution in the PARSED TREE, never the raw text (the serialiser escapes a quote or backslash by construction, so the credential cannot break out of its string; splicing into raw JSON would be an injection surface); bounded at `MAX_BODY_VAULT_REFS` = 8. **A pointer that cannot be addressed is a REFUSAL, not a silent skip** — leaving the reference in place would send the vault PATH, which names the provider and the user, to the third party. **The fast path costs one substring scan** of the raw bytes, which is every request the fleet makes today.
